For the right-hand side of (
2
), consider the six-vertex model configurations. Multiplying with
w^{-\binom{n}{2}}
results in dividing the weight of every bulk vertex by
w
. Taking the limit
w\to\infty
then excludes the local configurations

\bullet

and

\bullet

, since the respective weights tend to zero. The only six-vertex model configuration that avoids these two local configurations is the one that corresponds to the DSASM with
1
s along the antidiagonal and
0
s everywhere else, the weight of which is
\prod_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{n-1}
. An alternative way to obtain the limit of the right-hand side is to use the corresponding identity in (
2
) together with the following version of Schur’s Pfaffian identity
2
2

2

This identity follows from the classical Schur’s Pfaffian identity
\pf_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 2n}\left(\frac{x_{j}-x_{i}}{x_{i}+x_{j}}\right)=\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant 2n}\frac{x_{j}-x_{i}}{x_{i}+x_{j}}
after applying the transformation
x_{i}\mapsto\frac{1+x_{i}}{1-x_{i}}
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant 2n
. For the odd case, we additionally set
x_{2n}=1
and rearrange appropriately.

[
Sch11
]
:

\pf_{\chi_{\mathrm{even}}(n)\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left(\begin{cases}1,&i=0,\\
\frac{x_{j}-x_{i}}{1-x_{i}x_{j}},&i\geqslant 1\end{cases}\right)=\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{x_{j}-x_{i}}{1-x_{i}x_{j}}.

After dividing both sides by
\prod_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{n-1}
, we eventually obtain (
1
).

Another way to recover the classical Littlewood identity is by setting
w=0
, in which case Proposition
2.2

(ii)
implies that the left-hand side of (
2
) reduces to

\sum_{0\leqslant k_{1}<\dots<k_{n}}s_{(k_{n}-n+1,k_{n-1}-n+2,\dots,k_{1})}(x_{1},...,x_{n})\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(1+x_{i}x_{j})\\
=\sum_{\lambda}s_{\lambda}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(1+x_{i}x_{j}).

On the other hand, the right-hand side of (
2
) becomes

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-x_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1+x_{i}x_{j}}{1-x_{i}x_{j}}

due to the following transformation of Schur’s Pfaffian identity
3
3

3

In this case, we apply the transformation
x_{i}\mapsto\frac{x_{i}}{x_{i}^{2}-1}
for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant 2n
to the classical Schur’s Pfaffian identity.
:

\pf_{\chi_{\mathrm{even}}(n)\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left(\begin{cases}1,&i=0,\\
\frac{(x_{j}-x_{i})(1+x_{i}x_{j})}{(x_{i}+x_{j})(1-x_{i}x_{j})},&i\geqslant 1\end{cases}\right)=\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{(x_{j}-x_{i})(1+x_{i}x_{j})}{(x_{i}+x_{j})(1-x_{i}x_{j})}.

Dividing both sides by
\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(1+x_{i}x_{j})
yields the classical Littlewood identity (
1
).

The question is whether there are also combinatorial means to establish both sides. As for the left-hand side,

R_{(k_{1},...,k_{n})}^{\ast}(x_{1},...,x_{n};1,1,0)=s_{(k_{n}-n+1,k_{n-1}-n+2,\dots,k_{1})}(x_{1},...,x_{n})\prod\limits_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(1+x_{i}x_{j})

follows using Theorem
3.5
and Urban renewal as indicated in
[
Fis23
]
. Regarding the right-hand side, we need to show

(26)

\left.Z_{\DSASM}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\right|_{w=0}=\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(1+x_{i}x_{j}).

We modify the weights in Table
1
as follows: first we multiply the left boundary weights with
-\tau(x)
, which results in
total in an extra factor
(-1)^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\tau(x_{i})
of the partition function. By using the fact that, along

-shaped paths, there is one more occurrence of
1
than of
-1
, and by further multiplying by
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\tau(x_{i})
, we can ensure that the weights of the local configurations corresponding
to the matrix entries
-1
are equal to
1
. Finally, we can suppress the minus signs in the left boundary weights for

\bullet

and

\bullet

as there is the same number of such local configurations, see the proof of Proposition
5.3
. After setting
w=0
, we
obtain the weights provided in Table

: Building on the considerations presented in
[
ABF20
, Section 5.7]
, the partition function
Z_{\DSASM}(x_{1},\allowbreak\ldots,x_{n})
with respect to the weights in Table
4
is the weighted enumeration of the matchings of the edge-weighted graph sketched in Figure
1
, where all vertices are covered except for possibly some on the left boundary.

.
The partition function
Z_{\DSASM}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
with respect to the weights in Table
4
is given by the generating function of perfect matchings of the grid graph in
1(a)
(here for
n=5
), where the red vertices on the left boundary may remain uncovered. The corresponding edge weights are provided in
1(b)
.

We pose it as an open problem to provide a combinatorial proof that this weighted enumeration is
\prod_{1\leqslant i\leqslant j\leqslant n}(1+x_{i}x_{j})
, where the additional factor
\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1+x_{i}^{2})
appears compared to (
26
) due to the modification of weights. A natural question is whether
urban renewal
can be applied here as in the case of the Aztec diamond, see for instance
[
Pro03
]
.

8.
A conjectural generalization of Theorem
1.1
for fully inhomogeneous spin Hall–Littlewood polynomials

In this section, we present a conjectural Littlewood identity for fully inhomogeneous spin Hall–Littlewood polynomials that generalizes Theorem
1.1
, along with possible consequences including a new Littlewood identity for Hall–Littlewood polynomials. It is planned to study the identity for fully inhomogeneous spin Hall–Littlewood polynomials in a forthcoming paper.

Recall that, for a partition
\lambda
,
m_{r}(\lambda)
denotes the number of parts equal to
r
.

Conjecture 8.1
.

Let
n
be a positive integer and let
p
be a non-negative integer. Setting

\xi_{j}=1
for all
j
in
\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
, we conjecture

(27)

\sum_{\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant\lambda_{n}\geqslant 0}\prod_{r\geqslant 0}\frac{(-s_{r};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}{(q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})= \prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+q^{1/2}}{1-u_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1-qu_{i}u_{j}}{u_{i}-u_{j}}\\
\times\pf_{\chi_{\mathrm{even}}(n)\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left(\begin{cases}1,&i=0,\\
\frac{(u_{i}-u_{j})((1+q)(1-q^{1/2}u_{i}u_{j})+(u_{i}+u_{j})(q^{1/2}-q))}{(1+q^{1/2})(1-u_{i}u_{j})(1-qu_{i}u_{j})},&i\geqslant 1,\end{cases}\right)

where we set
s_{j}=s
for all
j\geqslant p
,
u_{i}=\frac{s+x_{i}}{1+sx_{i}}
for all
i
and consider
both sides as power series in
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}
.

The conjecture has been checked for
n=1
and arbitrary
p
, for
n=2
if
p=0,1
, and for
n=3,4
if
p=0
.

Next, we demonstrate that the case

s_{j}=-q^{-1/2}
is equivalent to Theorem
1.1
. Indeed, it holds

\sum_{\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant\lambda_{n}\geqslant 0}\prod_{r\geqslant 0}\frac{(q^{-1/2};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}{(q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}F_{\lambda}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})\\
=q^{\binom{n}{2}/2}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(q^{1/2}-x_{i})\\
\left.\times\sum_{0\leqslant\lambda_{n}<\lambda_{n-1}<\ldots<\lambda_{1}}R^{\ast}_{(\lambda_{n},\ldots,\lambda_{1})}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};1,1,-q^{1/2}-q^{-1/2})\right|_{x_{i}=\frac{u_{i}+q^{-1/2}}{1+q^{-1/2}u_{i}}}\\
=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+q^{1/2}}{1-u_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1-qu_{i}u_{j}}{u_{i}-u_{j}}\\
\times\pf_{\chi_{\mathrm{even}}(n)\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left(\begin{cases}1,&i=0,\\
\frac{(u_{i}-u_{j})((1+q)(1-q^{1/2}u_{i}u_{j})+(u_{i}+u_{j})(q^{1/2}-q))}{(1+q^{1/2})(1-u_{i}u_{j})(1-qu_{i}u_{j})},&i\geqslant 1,\end{cases}\right)

where we have used Theorem
1.1
for the last equality.
A similar calculation shows that the case
s_{j}=-q^{1/2}
of (
27
) is equivalent to Corollary
1.2
.

A consequence of Conjecture
8.1
would be a certain Littlewood identity for Hall–Littlewood polynomials. Recall that the Hall–Littlewood polynomials are defined as

P_{\lambda}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q)\coloneq(1-q)^{n}\prod_{r\geqslant 0}\frac{1}{(q;q)_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}\frac{\asym_{x_{1},...,x_{n}}\left[\prod\limits_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(x_{i}-qx_{j})\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{\lambda_{i}}\right]}{\prod\limits_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}(x_{i}-x_{j})}

and hence

\left.P_{\lambda}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q)=\prod_{r\geqslant 0}\frac{1}{(q;q)_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}F_{\lambda}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\right|_{\xi_{i}=1,s_{j}=0}.

Conjecture
8.1
would thus imply the following Littlewood identity for Hall–Littlewood polynomials

(28)

\sum_{\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant\lambda_{n}\geqslant 0}\prod_{r\geqslant 0}(-q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}P_{\lambda}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+q^{1/2}}{1-x_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1-qx_{i}x_{j}}{x_{i}-x_{j}}\\
\times\pf_{\chi_{\mathrm{even}}(n)\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left(\begin{cases}1,&i=0,\\
\frac{(x_{i}-x_{j})((1+q)(1-q^{1/2}x_{i}x_{j})+(x_{i}+x_{j})(q^{1/2}-q))}{(1+q^{1/2})(1-x_{i}x_{j})(1-qx_{i}x_{j})},&i\geqslant 1,\end{cases}\right)

where we have used

\frac{(q;q)_{m}}{(q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m}}=(-q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m}.

Note that the left-hand side of (
28
) is very similar to the left-hand side of the following Littlewood identity for Hall–Littlewood polynomials by Kawanaka
[
Kaw99
, (1.21)]
, which was essentially proved in
[
Kaw91
]
:

(29)

\sum_{\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant\lambda_{n}\geqslant 1}\prod_{r\geqslant 1}(-q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}P_{\lambda}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};q)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+q^{1/2}x_{i}}{1-x_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1-qx_{i}x_{j}}{1-x_{i}x_{j}}.

These identities differ in that Kawanaka’s identity sums over partitions allowing parts equal to zero, whereas the other sums over partitions with strictly positive parts.

Now, we present an even more general conjectural identity that involves an additional parameter
\gamma
, compare
with Gavrilova’s identity
[
Gav23
, (1)]
; it also includes Kawanaka’s identity as a special case.

Conjecture 8.2
.

Let
n
be a positive integers and
p
be a non-negative integer. Setting

\xi_{j}=1
for all
j
in
\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
, we conjecture

\sum_{\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant\lambda_{n}\geqslant 0}\frac{(-\gamma q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m_{0}(\lambda)}}{(q;q)_{m_{0}(\lambda)}}(-\gamma^{-1}s_{0};q^{1/2})_{m_{0}(\lambda)}\prod_{r\geqslant 1}\frac{(-s_{r};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}{(q^{1/2};q^{1/2})_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})\\
=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+q^{1/2}}{1-u_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1-qu_{i}u_{j}}{u_{i}-u_{j}}\\
\times\pf_{\chi_{\mathrm{even}}(n)\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\left(\begin{cases}1+(\gamma-1)\frac{(q^{1/2}-\gamma^{-1}s_{0})(1-u_{j})}{(1+q^{1/2})(1-s_{0}u_{j})},&i=0,\\
\frac{(u_{i}-u_{j})(((1+q)(1-q^{1/2}u_{i}u_{j})+(u_{i}+u_{j})(q^{1/2}-q))+(\gamma-1)f(u_{i},u_{j}))}{(1+q^{1/2})(1-u_{i}u_{j})(1-qu_{i}u_{j})},&i\geqslant 1,\end{cases}\right)

where

f(u,v)=\frac{(q^{1/2}-\gamma^{-1}s_{0})(1-uv)}{(1+q^{1/2})(1-s_{0}u)(1-s_{0}v)}\left((1+q^{1/2})(1+\gamma^{-1}s_{0})(1+q\gamma uv)\right.\\
\left.+(1+\gamma)(q-\gamma^{-1}s_{0})(1-q^{1/2}uv)-(1+\gamma)q^{1/2}(q^{1/2}+\gamma^{-1}s_{0})(u+v)\right),

and we set
s_{j}=s
for all
j\geqslant p
,
u_{i}=\frac{s+x_{i}}{1+sx_{i}}
for all
i
and consider
both sides as power series in
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}
.

This conjecture has also been checked for
n=1
, for
n=2
if
p=0,1
, and for
n=3,4
if
p=0
.

If we first set
s_{0}=0
and then
\gamma=0
, the right-hand side of the identity in Conjecture
8.2
simplifies to

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1+q^{1/2}u_{i}}{1-u_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1-qu_{i}u_{j}}{1-u_{i}u_{j}}.

Further setting
s_{r}=0
for all
r
leads to Kawanaka’s identity (
29
).

Finally, we present the following related Littlewood identity for fully inhomogeneous spin Hall-Littlewood polynomials, which we were unable to find in the literature. It was discovered in collaborative work with Moritz Gangl and its proof will appear in the above mentioned forthcoming paper related to Conjecture
8.2
.

Theorem 8.3
.

Let
n
be a positive integers and
p
be a non-negative integer. Setting

\xi_{j}=1
for all
j
in
\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
, we conjecture

\sum_{\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\geqslant\ldots\geqslant\lambda_{n}\geqslant 0}\prod_{r\geqslant 0}\frac{(-s_{r};q)_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}{(q;q)_{m_{r}(\lambda)}}\mathsf{F}_{\lambda}(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})= \prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{1-u_{i}}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{1-qu_{i}u_{j}}{1-u_{i}u_{j}},

where we set
s_{j}=s
for all
j\geqslant p
and
u_{i}=\frac{s+x_{i}}{1+sx_{i}}
for all
i
and consider
both sides as power series in
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}
.

It is a generalization of (1.4) in
[
Fis24
]
, which is obtained by setting
s_{x}=-q^{-1}
and
u_{i}=\frac{-1+qx_{i}}{q-x_{i}}
, and identifying
w=-q-1-q^{-1}
in
[
Fis24
, (1.4)]
.
